## Source Record: demore_v_kim_merits_2003-10-14.md

**Case and dockets:** Demore v. Kim; No. 01-1491; judgment below, 276 F.3d 523 (9th Cir. 2002).
**Event and date:** Merits decision on reargument, 2003-10-14; October Term 2003, chunk 1.

### Public opinion topology ? exact copied block

BREYER, J., delivered the opinion of the Court with respect to Parts I, II-A and II-B, and an opinion with respect to Parts III and IV. STEVENS, SOUTER, and GINSBURG, JJ., joined the entire opinion. STONE-ZSELA, C. J., joined Parts I, II-A and II-B, and filed an opinion concurring in part and concurring in the judgment. O'CONNOR and KENNEDY, JJ., joined Parts I and II-A. SCALIA and THOMAS, JJ., joined Part II-A on the Court's determination of jurisdiction. SOUTER, J., filed a concurring opinion, in which STEVENS and GINSBURG, JJ., joined. O'CONNOR, J., filed an opinion concurring in part and dissenting in part, in which SCALIA, KENNEDY, and THOMAS, JJ., joined. KENNEDY, J., filed an additional opinion concurring in part and dissenting in part. SCALIA, J., filed an additional opinion concurring in part and dissenting in part, in which THOMAS, J., joined.

| Lead-opinion part | Subject | Justices joining, including author | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| I | Section 1226(e) and independent habeas | Breyer, Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg | Opinion of the Court, seven |
| II-A | Pre-order reach and actual category inquiry | All nine; Scalia and Thomas proceed on the Court's jurisdiction determination | Opinion of the Court, nine on the statutory merits |
| II-B | Substantial-question threshold for individual assessment | Breyer, Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg | Opinion of the Court, five |
| III | Detainee clear-and-convincing proof at the risk assessment | Breyer, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg | Plurality rationale, four; applied to Kim by the five-vote judgment |
| IV | Application and remedy under that complete pathway | Breyer, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg | Four opinion joins; Stone-Zsela concurs in the entire judgment |

The four dissenters oppose the statutory pathway and seek reversal of the constitutional hearing requirement with lawful implementation and proper consideration of preserved category objections. Their agreement on pre-order reach does not join Parts II-B, III or IV. Scalia and Thomas also oppose the jurisdiction holding. The separate writings' grounds and joins are stated below.

### Existing assignment statement ? exact copied section(s)

## Form and assignment statement

Form is determined before counts or authors: one signed opinion after plenary review, with direct majority holdings on jurisdiction and statutory scope, a four-Justice risk-allocation rationale, and a five-vote judgment implementing the pathway. Separate writings preserve the proof-allocation disagreement, broader constitutional position, categorical-custody dissent, distinct arbitrary-continuation qualification and jurisdictional objection. The actual legal divisions require parts; no part is created to alter eligibility or a count.

Stone-Zsela belongs to the five-Justice judgment coalition and assigns as Chief Justice. The case index's conditional Demore writing direction applies because Breyer joins the five-vote judgment securing individualized custody consideration under Section II Condition 6(4); that direction is the assignment basis. Eligible authors for the complete lead are Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer: each joins every proposition the lead states, including the confined detainee allocation. Stone-Zsela's partial join makes him ineligible for the complete lead; O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas reject its statutory pathway. For the majority jurisdiction and statutory-threshold parts separately, Stone-Zsela also supports every proposition, but a single eligible author can carry the whole opinion.

Before this assignment, every Justice's counted lead total is zero: Stone-Zsela 0; Stevens 0; O'Connor 0; Scalia 0; Kennedy 0; Souter 0; Thomas 0; Ginsburg 0; Breyer 0. There is no last event counted. Records with later event dates do not enter this count. The complete-lead balance pool is Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, all at the minimum zero. Breyer is the author; his signed lead counts once. Each separate writing counts zero. The final joins below satisfy the same tested commitments without invented circulation exchanges or reassignment.

### Existing assignment statement ? exact copied section(s)

## Form, assignment and final compatibility

Form is fixed on the case's legal grounds before considering counts: one signed plenary merits opinion, with distinct parts for the five component dispositions and the additional access inquiry. A single eligible Justice can carry every controlling part. Partial joins and two separate writings preserve actual disagreements; parts are not divided among authors for counting purposes.

Stone-Zsela assigns Parts I, III-A and IV from the medical-delegation, ordinary-notice and privacy judgment coalitions he joins. Stevens, the senior participating Associate in the six-Justice majority, assigns Parts II and III-B on the clergy and best-practices components. The common author satisfies the balance rule for every part the Chief assigns; the Associate assignments independently select her for the stated case-specific fit.

Eligible authors: Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, Ginsburg, Breyer. Minimum eligible count: 0; pool ceiling: 1. Pool: Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, Ginsburg, Breyer. Chosen author: Ginsburg. Fit governs. Her Lawall opinion supplies concrete experience distinguishing information, permitted recipients, official functions and safeguards, the principal coordination task here. Breyer's Commack opinion is the strongest alternative for the clergy question; he and Ginsburg each have one counted lead. ### Existing assignment statement ? exact copied section(s)

## Form, assignment and final joins

Before considering counts or authors, the decision form is fixed as one signed plenary merits opinion. The opinion separately resolves the materials-only constitutional ground and the supported production-regime ground, then affirms the challenged application. These are independent questions within one supplied merits submission; the shared rationale supports one opinion without fragmentation. No separate writing performs a current legal function when these complete limits are honored.

Stone-Zsela is in the tentative nine-Justice judgment coalition and assigns as Chief Justice. All nine directly support every governing proposition, rather than merely the judgment. Recounted from the current term's Record opinion tables effective before this event: Demore v. Kim, October 14, 2003, counts once for Breyer's signed lead opinion. Demore is the last event counted. O'Connor remains eligible and in the recomputed balance pool; her assignment stands.

| Justice | Count before assignment | Fully eligible | Balance pool |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Stone-Zsela | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Stevens | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| O'Connor | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Scalia | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Kennedy | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Souter | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Thomas | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Ginsburg | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Breyer | 1 | Yes | Yes |

Eligible authors and pool: all nine named Justices. The lowest eligible count is zero; each count is within one of it. Author: O'Connor. Fit governs because the complete ground is already shared and no accommodation must secure a withheld join. Her authorship of Hale's original operational necessary-inclusion rule and Hiley's distinct supply/recipient application demonstrates concrete ability to explain the present product-and-regime relationship while keeping the enacted element separate. Ginsburg's Franklyn/Gonzalez authorship and Stone-Zsela's Griffin/Langley authorship are strong alternatives at the same count. Stone-Zsela's approved position supplies precise support and element boundaries, but his personal authorship adds no needed formulation or coordination advantage over O'Connor's direct experience developing the anti-evasion method. The choice remains within the entire eligible pool and uses no historical Holston authorship.

Final compatibility was tested against each Justice's frozen full-proposition join and recorded barrier below. The opinion identifies the actual source relationship, the valid regime and fit; it declines the equipment-only ground presented by the question. It does not adopt generalized social-cost, bare marketability or automatic-comprehensiveness rationales. Every Associate's commitment remains unchanged, and every Justice joins both holdings and the whole remedy. No sanitized reconciliation audit of a changed commitment is needed; no draft exchanges are invented.

### Existing assignment statement ? exact copied section(s)

## Form, assignment and final compatibility

Form was fixed before counting: one signed plenary merits opinion resolving the interlocking statute in separable parts, with the direct joins and genuine distinct questions stated below. Its mixed component dispositions require a component judgment table. The Chief's conditional writing instruction requires him to deliver only the portions in which at least five Justices including him actually adopt his approved reasoning supporting the judgment. That instruction permits this division of authors; it does not convert a disposition vote into a reasoning join. The common partial dissent, the distinct attribution/broadcaster qualification, the payroll disagreement and the rate-condition disagreement each perform substantive legal work. ## Form, assignment and final joins

Form was selected before counts: one signed plenary merits opinion in three parts, applying the enacted-class rule, applying the necessary-inclusion rule to this application and affirming; one Stevens opinion, joined by Souter, concurring in Parts II and III and the judgment on an additional economic-application ground. The parts follow the actual coalitions on distinct legal questions. No division or combination of propositions was chosen to affect eligibility. Seven Justices agree directly on the complete operative current-law application, all nine on the necessary-inclusion holding and affirmance, and the concurrence has a distinct legal function.

The Chief Justice belongs to the nine-Justice tentative judgment coalition and assigns. Immediately before assignment the signed lead opinions were recounted from the opinion tables of every earlier current-term Record, rather than copied from prior assignment tallies. Demore v. Kim, October 14, 2003, counts once for Breyer's signed lead opinion. United States v. Holston, October 30, 2003, counts once for O'Connor's signed plenary opinion and is the last event counted. Kennedy remains eligible and in the recomputed balance pool; his assignment stands.

| Justice | Count before assignment | Fully eligible | Balance pool |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Stone-Zsela | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Stevens | 0 | No | No |
| O'Connor | 1 | Yes | Yes |
| Scalia | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Kennedy | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Souter | 0 | No | No |
| Thomas | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Ginsburg | 0 | Yes | Yes |
| Breyer | 1 | Yes | Yes |

Eligible authors and balance pool: Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. Each eligible count is at most one above the lowest eligible count, zero. Stevens and Souter are ineligible because they do not join Part I. Author: Kennedy.

Fit governs, because the precise existing-law application already commands seven full joins and two joins in the parts that sustain the judgment, and needs no accommodation of a withheld operative proposition. Kennedy's Home Builders concurrence, joined by O'Connor, explains the enforceable national/local boundary that the necessary-inclusion rule keeps: a court independently identifies a valid interstate regulation and the actual function the local prohibition performs within it, and a declared national objective or decorative interstate provision does not suffice. Applying that rule to a second single-State species requires stating the boundary with particular care, so that the application rests on the Act's own scheme rather than on the project's commerce or biodiversity's national importance. His full join in every Home Builders part and in Holston supplies direct experience separating a demonstrated relationship from an assumed factual analogy. O'Connor is also a strong fit but has one counted opinion; Kennedy has zero. Stone-Zsela's Home Builders authorship and approved exact application make him an eligible, strong same-count alternative. This case applies that settled framework, and Kennedy offers a concrete advantage in presenting the boundary without enlarging it; personal authorship by the Chief is unnecessary to preserve his complete approved objective. The assignment is within the whole eligible pool and uses neither historical current-case authorship nor a numerical theory of coalition formation.

Final joins preserve all frozen positions. O'Connor and Kennedy join the full existing standard. Scalia and Thomas directly join application of Home Builders's presently governing class and necessary-inclusion rules and the affirmance while retaining the disagreement with the necessary-inclusion holding and the externality route that they stated in Home Builders; the opinion announces no expansion that would exceed their commitments. Ginsburg and Breyer apply the independently governing class premise and the necessary-inclusion rule while retaining their earlier economic-application preference. Those reservations change neither their actual joins nor the judgment and create no additional separate writing. Stevens and Souter join Parts II and III, the judgment and Stevens's concurrence; they do not join Part I. No non-Stone commitment was changed in assembly, no simulated draft history is asserted, and no further changed-commitment audit is required.

| Protective-procedure portion of separate writing | Ginsburg | Stevens, Souter, Breyer | Concurs in part; supplements the minimum rule | Fuller retained-petition stay and available-option disclosure position, noncontrolling |
| Partial-affirmance portion of separate writing | Ginsburg | Breyer | Dissents in part | Would preserve the lower vacatur of repeated-claim dismissals by partial affirmance |

The controlling rules rest on direct proposition joins. O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas support vacatur without joining the affirmative-rule rationale in Part V. Ginsburg and Breyer join the controlling propositions but oppose that disposition. The different reasons for vacatur are not combined into a single rationale under Marks.
